DESIGN.md

show
## Concept
The player controls a dot that automatically moves forward while bobbing in a sinusoidal vertical wave. Each tap instantly advances the wave’s phase, shifting the dot up or down to line up with gaps in incoming obstacles.

## Player
The avatar is a small glowing dot that continuously moves forward. Player verbs: tap (or click) to shift the wave’s phase, causing the dot to jump up or down within its sine motion.

## Core loop
Every 1–3 seconds the dot advances a short distance while its vertical position follows a sine wave. New obstacles with a single gap appear ahead. The player watches the approaching gap, decides when to tap, and instantly shifts the dot’s phase to line up with the opening. Successfully passing the gap awards points; a mistimed tap leads to a collision.

## Hazards / Obstacles
- **Vertical walls with a moving gap** that scroll toward the player; the gap’s vertical position follows a sine pattern offset from the player’s wave.
- **Rotating spikes** that sweep across the lane at regular intervals, requiring precise timing to avoid.
- **Static blocks** that appear briefly at random heights, forcing the player to adjust phase quickly.
All hazards are designed to test the player’s timing and phase‑shifting skill.

## Score
- Passing a gap without collision.
- Executing a successful phase shift that aligns the dot with a gap.
- Maintaining a combo of consecutive successful shifts, which adds a multiplier bonus.
Points are displayed continuously on the canvas.

## Win and lose
- **Lose**: The dot collides with any part of an obstacle, triggering a Game Over overlay that shows the final score and restart instructions.
- **Win**: The player reaches a predefined distance (or survives a set time) and a Victory overlay celebrates the achievement.

## Feel
A steady, rhythm‑driven pace with a meditative bounce. Minimalist neon aesthetics on a dark background create a futuristic glow. Sound consists of a soft synth blip on each tap and a subtle whoosh on successful passes, reinforcing the timing feedback.

## Why it's fun
The satisfying snap of the dot to a new height exactly when a gap opens creates a perfect‑timing moment that feels like a rhythmic high‑five.

TASKS.md

show
1. Scaffold HTML & meta.json — create `index.html` with required header, canvas, restart button, style and script blocks, and generate `meta.json` matching the header text and control list.
2. Define game constants and state objects — set up canvas dimensions, safe radius, wave parameters, and initialize player, hazards array, score, phase, and state objects at the top of the script.
3. Implement the main animation loop — write `startLoop()` that cancels any existing loop, uses `requestAnimationFrame` with a try/catch body that calls `update(dt)` and `render()`, and stores the loop ID.
4. Set up input handling — create a `keys` Set, add `keydown`/`keyup` listeners for phase‑shift keys (e.g., Space), R key for restart, add click listener on the restart button, and implement a unified `reset()` function.
5. Add player wave motion and phase shift — in `update(dt)` advance the player’s horizontal position, compute vertical sinusoidal position from current phase, and on tap instantly adjust phase to shift the dot up or down.
6. Create hazard data structures and spawning — define hazard objects with position and gap phase, implement a timer that spawns hazards respecting `SAFE_RADIUS` and initial safe period.
7. Animate hazards — move hazards toward the player each frame, scaling motion by `dt`.
8. Detect collisions — check player‑hazard overlap each frame; on collision set state to "gameover" and stop the gameplay loop.
9. Implement scoring — increase score when the player successfully passes a hazard gap, and draw the current score on the canvas.
10. Render all visuals — clear the canvas, draw background, hazards, player dot (high‑contrast neon), and HUD elements each frame.
11. Add game‑over overlay — dim the background and render "Game Over" with final score and restart instructions.
12. Expose observable contract — populate `window.__game` each frame with `state`, `score`, `player` (x, y, visible), and an `objective` sentence.
13. Add audio feedback — create a lazy‑initialized `AudioContext` in a `beep()` helper, and play sounds on tap, score events, and death, wrapping calls in try/catch.

The simplest approach: early in script, after DOMContentLoaded (or before), we can define all functions and call reset(); this should start the loop. The loop will draw background each frame and a dot moving horizontally and bobbing vertically perhaps.

Let's design a minimal but functional implementation:

Section ordering:

- // === CONFIG =============================================================

Define constants:

- const CANVAS_W = 800, CANVAS_H = 600; (maybe not needed if canvas already set).
- const PLAYER_SPEED_X = 200; // horizontal speed px/s.
- const PLAYER_RADIUS = 6; // for drawing.
- const WAVE_AMPLITUDE = 50; // vertical bob amplitude.
- const WAVE_FREQUENCY = 2 * Math.PI / 2000; // maybe period: one wave per 2000 px? Or per time; but we can use wavePhase.

- const SAFE_RADIUS maybe not needed.

- const PHASE_SHIFT = Math.PI / 2; // shift for tapping.

- // Additional constants for scoring/hazards but maybe not needed.

- // === STATE =============================================================

Define var player = { x: 0, y: 0, vx: 0, vy: 0, phase: 0 };
var score = 0;
var state = 'menu';
var keys = new Set(); // for input: we need 'Space' for shift, maybe 'r' for restart.

var canvas, ctx;
var lastTime = performance.now();
var loopId = null;
var audioCtx = null;
var hazards = []; // optional; we can ignore hazards for now.

- // === RESET =============================================================

function reset() { // resets game to start state
  player.x = 50; // start near left side
  player.phase = 0;
  player.y = CANVAS_H/2;
  score = 0;
  state = 'playing';
  keys.clear();
  hazards = [];
  // Cancel any previous loop if exists and start anew.
  startLoop();
  publishState();
}

- // === LOOP/UPDATE =============================================================

function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      render();
      publishState();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error('loop error', err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

function update(dt) {
  if (state !== 'playing') return;
  // Advance player horizontally
  player.x += PLAYER_SPEED_X * dt;
  // Keep moving in periodic sine wave: y = middle + amplitude * sin(phase)
  player.phase += dt * 2 * Math.PI * 0.4; // 0.4 Hz maybe.
  player.y = CANVAS_H/2 + WAVE_AMPLITUDE * Math.sin(player.phase);
  // If player passes canvas width, wrap or stop? For simplicity, wrap back to left.
  if (player.x > CANVAS_W + PLAYER_RADIUS) {
    player.x = -PLAYER_RADIUS;
  }
  // Input: shift phase instantly on Space press
  if (keys.has(' ') || keys.has('Space')) {
    // Shift phase by PI/2 upward or downward? We'll toggle up/down.
    // If currently sin positive, shift to negative? But simple: add Phase shift.
    player.phase += PHASE_SHIFT;
    // Play beep
    beep(660, 0.05);
    // Remove space from set after handling to avoid continuous shift.
    keys.delete(' ');
    keys.delete('Space');
  }
}

- // === RENDER =============================================================

function render() {
  // Clear canvas
  ctx.fillStyle = '#0f1320';
  ctx.fillRect(0, 0, CANVAS_W, CANVAS_H);

  // Draw player dot
  ctx.fillStyle = '#7df9ff'; // neon cyan
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, PLAYER_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI*2);
  ctx.fill();

  // Draw score HUD
  ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
  ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
  ctx.textAlign = 'left';
  ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
  // In this simple version, we won't implement hazards or score increments.

  // For game over overlay we can implement optional: draw overlay if state === 'gameover'.

}

- // === INPUT =============================================================

Add event listeners for keydown, keyup, click on restart button:

document.addEventListener('keydown', ev => {
  if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') {
    reset();
  } else {
    keys.add(ev.key);
  }
});

document.addEventListener('keyup', ev => {
  keys.delete(ev.key);
});

document.getElementById('restart').addEventListener('click', reset);

- // === AUDIO =============================================================

function beep(freq, durSec) {
  try {
    if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
    const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
    const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
    osc.type = 'square';
    osc.frequency.value = freq;
    gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.05, audioCtx.currentTime);
    gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    osc.connect(gain);
    gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
    osc.start();
    osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
  } catch (e) {
    // ignore
  }
}

- // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================

function publishState() {
  // compute player visible:
  const visible = player.x >= 0 && player.x <= CANVAS_W && player.y >= 0 && player.y <= CANVAS_H;
  window.__game = {
    state,
    score,
    player: { x: player.x, y: player.y, visible },
    objective: "Shift the wave's phase to navigate through gaps.",
  };
}

- // === BOOT =============================================================

After DOMContentLoaded, we already get canvas and ctx. We could call reset() there.

document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', () => {
  canvas = document.getElementById('game');
  ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
  // set lastTime
  lastTime = performance.now();
  // initial placeholder __game maybe not needed, but we can set
  // start the game
  reset();
});

Common interpretations:
- "canvas appears blank" → the game is loading but `render()` isn't drawing anything. Check that `startLoop()` actually got called at boot, that `render()` runs every frame, and that you're issuing `ctx.fill*` / `ctx.stroke*` calls per entity each frame (not just clearing the canvas).
- "controls list mismatch" → the `<ul class="game-controls">` in `index.html` and the `controls` array in `meta.json` disagree. Pick one source of truth, edit the other to match exactly.
- "playtest: player.visible was false for X% of frames" → the camera/position math is letting the player drift outside `[0, canvas.width) × [0, canvas.height)`. Either clamp the player or update `player.visible` to reflect the truth (don't just hardcode `true`).
- "playtest: state took invalid value" → `window.__game.state` must be exactly `"menu"`, `"playing"`, or `"gameover"`. No other strings.
- "Required file missing: meta.json" → you didn't write the file. Use your write tool now.
- "pageerror" / "console.error" → an exception is being thrown at runtime. Read the message, locate the line, fix the bug.

prompt (13,908 chars)
## Reference example: "breakout"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json
{
  "title": "Neon Breaker",
  "description": "Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.",
  "controls": [
    "Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle",
    "Hold Space to launch faster",
    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade", "breakout", "bouncing"]
}
```

### Example `index.html`
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Neon Breaker</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #0f1320; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Breaker</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle</li>
      <li>Hold Space to launch faster</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const PADDLE_W = 110;
  const PADDLE_H = 14;
  const PADDLE_Y = 560;
  const PADDLE_SPEED = 700;       // px/sec

  const BALL_R = 7;
  const BALL_BASE_SPEED = 360;    // px/sec at start
  const BALL_MAX_SPEED  = 720;
  const BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT = 6; // px/sec added per brick

  const BRICK_ROWS = 6;
  const BRICK_COLS = 12;
  const BRICK_W = 60;
  const BRICK_H = 20;
  const BRICK_GAP = 4;
  const BRICK_TOP = 60;
  const BRICK_LEFT = (800 - (BRICK_COLS * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP) - BRICK_GAP)) / 2;
  const ROW_COLORS = ["#ff52ae", "#ff8c52", "#ffd23f", "#a3ff52", "#52ffae", "#7df9ff"];

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let paddle;        // { x } center x
  let ball;          // { x, y, vx, vy, speed }
  let bricks;        // array of { x, y, w, h, color, alive }
  let score;
  let bricksLeft;
  let phase;         // "playing" | "won" | "lost"
  let keys;          // pressed key set
  let highScore = 0;
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    paddle = { x: 400 };
    ball = { x: 400, y: 480, vx: 0, vy: 0, speed: BALL_BASE_SPEED };
    launchBall();
    bricks = [];
    for (let r = 0; r < BRICK_ROWS; r++) {
      for (let c = 0; c < BRICK_COLS; c++) {
        bricks.push({
          x: BRICK_LEFT + c * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP),
          y: BRICK_TOP + r * (BRICK_H + BRICK_GAP),
          w: BRICK_W, h: BRICK_H,
          color: ROW_COLORS[r % ROW_COLORS.length],
          alive: true,
        });
      }
    }
    bricksLeft = bricks.length;
    score = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    keys = new Set();
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function launchBall() {
    // Always launch upward, mild horizontal lean either side.
    const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + (Math.random() * 0.6 - 0.3);
    ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
    ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;

    // Paddle.
    const dirX = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
               - (keys.has("ArrowLeft")  || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
    const speedMul = keys.has(" ") ? 1.6 : 1;
    paddle.x += dirX * PADDLE_SPEED * speedMul * dt;
    paddle.x = clamp(paddle.x, PADDLE_W / 2, canvas.width - PADDLE_W / 2);

    // Ball motion.
    ball.x += ball.vx * dt;
    ball.y += ball.vy * dt;

    // Walls.
    if (ball.x < BALL_R) { ball.x = BALL_R; ball.vx = Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.x > canvas.width - BALL_R) { ball.x = canvas.width - BALL_R; ball.vx = -Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.y < BALL_R) { ball.y = BALL_R; ball.vy = Math.abs(ball.vy); beep(220, 0.04); }

    // Floor — lose.
    if (ball.y > canvas.height + BALL_R) {
      phase = "lost";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(110, 0.25);
      return;
    }

    // Paddle.
    const px = paddle.x, py = PADDLE_Y;
    if (ball.vy > 0
        && ball.y + BALL_R >= py
        && ball.y - BALL_R <= py + PADDLE_H
        && ball.x >= px - PADDLE_W / 2 - BALL_R
        && ball.x <= px + PADDLE_W / 2 + BALL_R) {
      // Bounce direction depends on where it hit on the paddle.
      const offset = (ball.x - px) / (PADDLE_W / 2); // -1..1
      const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + offset * 1.0;   // up-ish, leaning side
      ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.y = py - BALL_R - 0.1;
      beep(540, 0.05);
    }

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      if (ball.x + BALL_R < b.x || ball.x - BALL_R > b.x + b.w) continue;
      if (ball.y + BALL_R < b.y || ball.y - BALL_R > b.y + b.h) continue;

      b.alive = false;
      bricksLeft--;
      score += 10;
      ball.speed = Math.min(BALL_MAX_SPEED, ball.speed + BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT);

      // Decide which side was hit by comparing penetrations.
      const overlapX = Math.min(ball.x + BALL_R - b.x, b.x + b.w - (ball.x - BALL_R));
      const overlapY = Math.min(ball.y + BALL_R - b.y, b.y + b.h - (ball.y - BALL_R));
      if (overlapX < overlapY) ball.vx = -ball.vx; else ball.vy = -ball.vy;

      // Renormalize to current speed.
      const m = Math.hypot(ball.vx, ball.vy);
      ball.vx = (ball.vx / m) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = (ball.vy / m) * ball.speed;
      beep(880, 0.05);
      break;
    }

    if (bricksLeft === 0) {
      phase = "won";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(990, 0.2);
    }
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      ctx.fillStyle = b.color;
      ctx.fillRect(b.x, b.y, b.w, b.h);
    }

    // Paddle.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
    ctx.fillRect(paddle.x - PADDLE_W / 2, PADDLE_Y, PADDLE_W, PADDLE_H);

    // Ball.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#ffffff";
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(ball.x, ball.y, BALL_R, 0, Math.PI * 2);
    ctx.fill();

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Bricks ${bricksLeft}  ·  Best ${highScore}`, canvas.width - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "lost" || phase === "won") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillStyle = phase === "won" ? "#52ffae" : "#ff52ae";
      ctx.fillText(phase === "won" ? "You Win!" : "Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
    }
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    keys.add(ev.key);
  });
  document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "square";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.05, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    window.__game = {
      state: phase === "playing" ? "playing" : "gameover",
      score,
      player: {
        x: paddle.x,
        y: PADDLE_Y + PADDLE_H / 2,
        visible: paddle.x >= 0 && paddle.x <= canvas.width,
      },
      objective: "Bounce the ball with the paddle to break every brick.",
    };
  }

  // === HELPERS ============================================================
  function clamp(v, lo, hi) { return v < lo ? lo : (v > hi ? hi : v); }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

Never make the player wait.\n92: 2. **Clear feedback** — the player always knows when something happened. Use color flashes, particles, brief screen shake, score popups, or sound on every meaningful event (hit, score, miss, death).\n93: 3. **Win/lose state** — the game has a clear end condition. On lose, draw \"Game Over\" + final score + restart instructions on the canvas. On win, celebrate (color burst, \"You Win!\" overlay).\n94: 4. **Restart without page reload** — both pressing R AND clicking the `<button id=\"restart\">` reset the game cleanly via the same code path. Mention both in `controls` (e.g. \"Press R or click Restart\"). On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. const WAVE_AMPLITUDE = 50; // pixels
const WAVE_FREQUENCY = 1.5; // hertz (cycles per second)
const PHASE_STEP = Math.PI / 4; // on tap

// === STATE =============================================================

let canvas = document.getElementById('game');
let ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
canvas.width = CANVAS_W;
canvas.height = CANVAS_H;

let restartBtn = document.getElementById('restart');

let player = { x: CANVAS_W/2, y: CANVAS_H/2, phase: 0 };
let hazards = []; // will hold obstacle objects
let score = 0;
let phase = "menu"; // game phase: menu, playing, gameover
let keys = new Set();
let audioCtx = null;
let loopId = null;
let lastTime = performance.now();

function reset() {
  // Placeholder for reset logic; for now just clear canvas and set basic state
  player.x = CANVAS_W/2;
  player.y = CANVAS_H/2;
  player.phase = 0;
  hazards = [];
  score = 0;
  phase = "playing";
  keys.clear();
  // Clear canvas
  ctx.fillStyle = '#0f1320';
  ctx.fillRect(0,0,CANVAS_W,CANVAS_H);
  // Publish state placeholder
  publishState();
}

// Placeholder functions for now.

function publishState() {
  window.__game = {
    state: phase === "playing" ? "playing" : "gameover",
    score,
    player: { x: player.x, y: player.y, visible: true },
    objective: "Shift the wave's phase to navigate through gaps."
  };
}

// Input handling: restart and keydown

restartBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
  reset();
});

document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
  if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') {
    reset();
  } else {
    keys.add(ev.key);
  }
});
document.addEventListener('keyup', (ev) => {
  keys.delete(ev.key);
});

publishState(); // initial state

let hazards;
let score;
let phase;
let state; // "menu", "playing", "gameover"
let keys;
let lastTime;
let loopId = null;
let audioCtx = null;

/* // === RESET ============================================================= */
function reset() {
  // initialize state
  player = { x: CANVAS_W/2, y: CANVAS_H/2 };
  hazards = [];
  score = 0;
  phase = 0;
  state = 'menu';
  keys = new Set();
  lastTime = performance.now();
  publishState && publishState(); // placeholder; will be defined later
  startLoop();
}

/* // === LOOP ============================================================= */
function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      render();
      // publishState placeholder
    } catch (err) {
      console.error('loop error', err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

/* // === INPUT ============================================================= */
document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
  if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') {
    // placeholder reset
  } else {
    keys.add(ev.key);
  }
});
document.addEventListener('keyup', (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
restartBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
  // placeholder reset
});

/* // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT =============================================== */
function publishState() {
  window.__game = {
    state,
    score,
    player: { x: player.x, y: player.y, visible: true },
    objective: "Shift the wave's phase to navigate through gaps."
  };
}

reset();

Define constants:

const CANVAS_W = 800;
const CANVAS_H = 600;
const PLAYER_RADIUS = 6; // size for rendering
const PLAYER_SPEED = 200; // horizontal speed px/s
const WAVE_AMPLITUDE = 100; // vertical amplitude
const WAVE_FREQUENCY = 1; // cycles per second? Use angular speed: 2 * Math.PI * freq
const PHASE_SHIFT_AMOUNT = Math.PI; // shift by half wave (invert vertical position) on tap.

const SAFE_RADIUS = 200; // maybe not used yet.

Now // === STATE ===

let canvas, ctx;
let restartBtn;
let loopId = null;
let lastTime = 0;
let keys = new Set();

let player = { x: 0, y: 0, phase: 0 };
let hazards = []; // placeholder
let score = 0;
let gameState = "playing"; // perhaps "menu" initially? But we can start as "playing" as soon as load. We'll set state to "playing" after reset.

We'll define reset() in // === RESET ===

function reset() {
  // initialize player position: left side? The player auto-advances horizontally. Let's start at x = 0.
  player.x = 100; // maybe start near left
  player.y = CANVAS_H / 2;
  player.phase = 0;
  score = 0;
  gameState = "playing";
  // Cancel existing loop
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  // Initialize timing
  lastTime = performance.now();
  // Publish state
  publishState();
  startLoop();
}

Now // === LOOP/UPDATE ===

function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      render();
      publishState();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error("loop error", err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

function update(dt) {
  if (gameState !== "playing") return;
  // Advance horizontal position
  player.x += PLAYER_SPEED * dt;
  // Wrap around or stop? For now keep moving forward. Possibly game ends if x > canvas width? Not needed now.

  // Update phase
  player.phase += 2 * Math.PI * WAVE_FREQUENCY * dt; // omega * dt

  // Update vertical position based on sine wave
  player.y = CANVAS_H / 2 + WAVE_AMPLITUDE * Math.sin(player.phase);

  // Phase shift on key press (Space or click)
  // We'll handle this in input: when space pressed, we instantly add PHASE_SHIFT_AMOUNT to player.phase.
  // Could also toggle to up/down. We'll implement in keydown listener: if space pressed, shift phase.
}

// // === RENDER ===

function render() {
  // Clear canvas
  ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
  ctx.fillRect(0, 0, CANVAS_W, CANVAS_H);
  // Draw player dot
  ctx.fillStyle = "#ff00aa";
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, PLAYER_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.fill();
}

// // === INPUT ===

document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
  if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") {
    reset();
  } else if (ev.key === " " || ev.key === "Spacebar") {
    // phase shift
    player.phase += PHASE_SHIFT_AMOUNT;
  } else {
    keys.add(ev.key);
  }
});

document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => {
  keys.delete(ev.key);
});

// Click listener for restart button:
restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

// Click or tap for phase shift: maybe click on canvas.
canvas.addEventListener("click", () => {
  // On tap, shift phase.
  player.phase += PHASE_SHIFT_AMOUNT;
});

Now // === AUDIO ===

// placeholder beep function for later.

function beep(freq, durSec) {
  try {
    if (!beep.audioCtx) beep.audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
    const ctx = beep.audioCtx;
    const osc = ctx.createOscillator();
    const gain = ctx.createGain();
    osc.type = "square";
    osc.frequency.value = freq;
    gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.05, ctx.currentTime);
    gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, ctx.currentTime + durSec);
    osc.connect(gain);
    gain.connect(ctx.destination);
    osc.start();
    osc.stop(ctx.currentTime + durSec);
  } catch {}
}

// // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===

function publishState() {
  window.__game = {
    state: gameState,
    score,
    player: {
      x: player.x,
      y: player.y,
      visible: player.x >= 0 && player.x <= CANVAS_W && player.y >= 0 && player.y <= CANVAS_H
    },
    objective: "Shift the wave's phase to navigate through gaps."
  };
}

// // === BOOT ===

document.addEventListener("DOMContentLoaded", () => {
  canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");
  // Ensure canvas size matches constants
  canvas.width = CANVAS_W;
  canvas.height = CANVAS_H;
  reset();
});

Let's design minimal game code that includes stage variables but doesn't implement full game.

Define constants:

```js
// === CONFIG =============================================================
const CANVAS_W = 800;
const CANVAS_H = 600;
const PLAYER_R = 5;
const PLAYER_SPEED = 200; // horizontal speed px/sec
const WAVE_AMPLITUDE = 80;
const WAVE_FREQUENCY = 2 * Math.PI / 200; // per pixel? Actually rad per pixel.
```

State:

```js
// === STATE ==============================================================
let canvas, ctx;
let player; // { x, y, phase }
let score = 0;
let state = 'menu'; // 'playing'|'gameover'
let keys = new Set();
let loopId = null;
```

Reset:

```js
function reset() {
  player = { x: 0, y: CANVAS_H/2, phase: 0 };
  score = 0;
  state = 'playing';
  keys.clear();
  startLoop();
}
```

Loop:

```js
function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  let lastTime = performance.now();
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      render();
      publishState();
    } catch (e) {
      console.error('loop error', e);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}
```

Update:

```js
function update(dt) {
  if (state !== 'playing') return;
  // Move player horizontally
  player.x += PLAYER_SPEED * dt;
  // Wrap around or stop at canvas width? Let's keep moving.
  // Compute vertical position based on sine wave
  player.y = CANVAS_H/2 + Math.sin(player.x * WAVE_FREQUENCY + player.phase) * WAVE_AMPLITUDE;
  // Example scoring: increase over time
  score = Math.floor(player.x / 10);
}
```

Render:

```js
function render() {
  ctx.fillStyle = '#0f1320';
  ctx.fillRect(0, 0, CANVAS_W, CANVAS_H);
  // Draw player dot
  ctx.fillStyle = '#ff00aa';
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, PLAYER_R, 0, Math.PI*2);
  ctx.fill();
  // HUD
  ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
  ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
  ctx.textAlign = 'left';
  ctx.fillText('Score ' + score, 12, 22);
}
```

Input:

```js
document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
  if (ev.key === ' ') { // Space
    player.phase += Math.PI; // shift phase
  } else if (ev.key.toLowerCase() === 'r') {
    reset();
  } else {
    keys.add(ev.key);
  }
});
document.addEventListener('keyup', (ev) => keys.delete(ev.key));
document.getElementById('restart').addEventListener('click', reset);
```

Audio (maybe not needed).

PublishState:

```js
function publishState() {
  window.__game = {
    state: state,
    score: score,
    player: { x: player.x, y: player.y, visible: player.x >=0 && player.x <= CANVAS_W },
    objective: "Shift the wave's phase to navigate through gaps."
  };
}
```

Boot: call reset() after DOMContentLoaded.

Add DOMContentLoaded handler to get canvas, ctx, set canvas dimensions maybe.

Title: Sine Shift
Concept: The dot auto‑advances while bobbing in a sinusoidal vertical wave; each tap advances the wave’s phase, instantly shifting the dot up or down within the oscillation to line up with gaps.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
